Bodhicaryavatara of Santideva edited by Vidhushekhara Bhattacharya

The Bodhicaryavatara states: "My mind is inclined towards this. It is skillful to cultivate the momentum of grace. If one is not able to discern the essence of the teachings, how can one expect to achieve the purpose of human life? If one does not engage in the practice, how can there be a gathering of merit? Just as the sun dispels the darkness of the night, the light of wisdom reveals the truth to the world."

BODHICARYAVATARA 3 Therefore, the mind should be engaged in the practice of virtue, for the power of sin is indeed extremely terrible. It is known by another, by what means the mind of enlightenment is attained, if not through the practice of virtue. Thus, one should contemplate the teachings of the great sage, for they reveal the ultimate happiness that transcends all suffering. The sufferings of existence are indeed numerous, and even those who desire happiness are caught in the web of suffering. Therefore, one should strive to cultivate the mind of enlightenment, freeing oneself from the bondage of desires.

The fruit of the Bodhi tree is attained by those who are skilled in all things. It constantly bears fruit and does not lead to rebirth, but rather to the contemplation of enlightenment. Even the most terrible sins, when heard, are overcome by the refuge of the brave. What then can the ignorant do? In the end, the flames of sin are extinguished by the water of virtue. Those who follow the teachings of the wise are blessed with happiness.

The mind of enlightenment should be understood in two ways: the mind of the aspiration for enlightenment and the mind of the realization of enlightenment. 15 Just as there is a distinction between the one who wishes to go and the one who actually goes, so too is there a distinction in the mind of the learned regarding the means of enlightenment. 16 The result of the mind of aspiration for enlightenment is great even in the world, for it does not become severed from merit, just as the mind of the one who has set out does not become severed from the path. 17 Therefore, when the mind is engaged in the pursuit of liberation, it does not waver due to the nature of its engagement. 18

The Bodhicaryavatara states: "Then, in response to the many of the mind of the one who is not distracted, the streams of merit are uninterrupted, like the sky. [4] The one who reads this text will attain the state of a Bodhisattva. The one who gives a good teaching, with the support of the teachings, will attain liberation. [20] The one who is free from suffering, who has the qualities of a Bodhisattva, will be able to help others. [29] The one who is mindful of the suffering of beings will not be disturbed by the pain of others. The wise one will think of the suffering of beings as a single entity. [22] What then is the immeasurable pain of a single being? The immeasurable suffering of beings is to be considered as one."

In the realms of the gods, fragrant incense and wish-fulfilling trees abound, as well as lakes and rivers filled with pure water. The trees are of the same nature as those that bear crops and other offerings worthy of worship. All the various plants and herbs are gathered together. Taking from the great sages, I shall offer these to the worthy ones.

I am devoid of merit; I do not possess anything. For the sake of my own benefit, I do not think of others. Why is that? I do not have any good qualities. I give myself to the Jinas, to all beings and their offspring. May the beings who are my protectors take care of me; I approach them as a servant. Through searching, I find the essence of the teachings. I am indeed in the state of being protected by your virtue; I do not commit any other sins. I do not engage in any other sinful actions.

BODHICARYAVATARA In the beautiful pillars of the radiant abode of Sukrāma, in the fragrant bathing places, there are those who are like the bright crystal. I perform the bathing of those Tathāgatas and their offspring. I give them the best of offerings, the nectar of the gods.

The Bodhicaryavatara, in the divine realm, I offer homage to Samantabhadra, the conqueror of the world, and to the great Avalokiteshvara. May I dwell in the assembly of the enlightened ones. 12 His son, the infinite one, I honor. 22 I will apply the finest fragrance of the most excellent scents. I will illuminate the world with the brilliance of the eight-fold path. 14 I will not forget the names of the great beings. 2 I will offer garlands of Mandara flowers, adorned with sweet fragrances. 15 I will dedicate myself to the delightful abodes of the birds.

The Bodhicaryavatara states that the one who comes does not harm me. The actions performed by the ignorant lead to various sins. At the time of rest, one who is destined to die does not perish; indeed, I will not die. Even when lying on the bed, one who remains is still bound. Only I alone must endure the pain of separation. The messenger of Yama, where is the relative, and where is the good? At that time, I was not served.

24 (11") BODHICARYAVATARA The impermanent, being devoid of attachment, does not know this fear. By the heedless one, I have not accumulated much sin. 42 What is the use of this? The one who is afflicted by great suffering is like a person who is wrapped in a blanket. 44 What then, is the purpose of this? The messenger is established. The person afflicted by great fever is like a person wrapped in a blanket. 44 With the gaze directed in all four directions, who is there to protect me from this fear? 45 Thus, the family resides here.

In the direction of the void, I see again the state of calmness and restraint. What then shall I do in that great fear? I take refuge in the Lord of the Universe, the one who is powerful. I take refuge in the words spoken by the world. They too have attained the fear of worldly existence. I take refuge in the Bodhisattva assembly with the right intention. I give my own self to the one who is aware of fear. Again, I give my own self to my own self.

BODHICARYAVATARA 29 Compassion alone is my thought, always directed towards the moon. How then can I let go of the inevitable suffering? 1) I am aware of the suffering of beings, and I have not committed any evil. Whatever is done with wisdom is indeed worthy of praise. I will not abandon this path. Om, I will guide those who are ahead. 165 With folded hands, I repeatedly bow down. 64 Smiling, I will not let go of this. Those who are steadfast in their vows are not to be disturbed. This is not a good path, nor is it a source of joy for me. 65 [19] I will not abandon the essence of my being.

BODHICARYAVATARA I offer my prostrations to all the fully awakened ones. I request the attention of those who have fallen into the pit of delusion and suffering. I request the beings who are in the state of suffering to be free from this world of darkness. Thus, may all beings attain the peace of liberation from all suffering. By this, may all beings be free from all forms of suffering.

38 BODHICARYAVATARA QS: The bridge for crossing the difficult is the general path for all beings. The sun of the world rises, illuminating the moon of enlightenment. 20: The great ocean of the world is not devoid of light. 21: The one who has risen from the darkness of ignorance is the one who has attained the state of enlightenment. 22: The one who is engaged in the path of happiness, whether they are a seeker or a practitioner, is the one who has reached the ultimate goal.

The Bodhicaryavatara states: "One who knows this inconceivable path of action, even if they are endowed with the mind of enlightenment, liberates those beings." Thus, the Bodhisattva, having attained this, is free from all obstacles. Therefore, they do not seek their own benefit. Even if another performs virtuous deeds, there is no way for them to attain the same state as the one who is enlightened. Even if one being is harmed, what can be said about the suffering of those who are numerous?

BODHICARYAVATARA 43 When will I attain the state of being a human? The Bodhicaryavatara teaches that the mind is not disturbed, like one who is delighted by mantras. I do not know who is standing at the end. The wise do not become attached to the pleasures of the senses. The wise do not become angry; how can they be attached to servitude? Those who are established in meditation are indeed free from disturbances. There, I do not become angry; I remain in a state of equanimity. All gods and humans, if they are wise, are also capable of not being bound by attachment.

The eternal name of the enemy may exist between the one who is not liberated. For again, the power that is gathered does not lead to the path of destruction. The mind that is not engaged in the pursuit of fear is indeed the one that is restrained. My effort is solely for the sake of wisdom; the wise ones are those who attain insight. As the restless one is situated in the middle, thus the one situated in the middle of the duality always creates a wound. The wound caused by the pain of the duality does not arise from the collision of the aggregates. In the place of enjoyment, even if one is disturbed by others, the mind does not get divided even in the midst of the crowd. May my desires perish, and may the offerings be given to the body. Let not the mind be disturbed at any time, even if the skill is lost.

The Bodhicaryavatara states: Those who, with great effort, overcome the obstacles of the enemy, are indeed the victorious heroes; the rest are merely the defeated. The qualities of suffering arise from the intensity of desire, and compassion is born from the suffering of beings. In the presence of anger, even in the midst of great suffering, what is the use of anger towards those who are also afflicted? When one is provoked, even if one is restrained, anger can still arise. Just as a seed, when pressed, can sprout, so too can anger arise even when one is restrained.

102 [41. BODHICARYAVATARA] This section discusses the nature of attachment and the consequences of desire. If something is born that is dear, what will happen again? The Bodhicaryavatara states: "As for me, I do not wish to be dependent on others; how can I act with respect towards them while not being in a servile position? Therefore, I declare the suffering caused by the suffering of beings. Today, I acknowledge the sin. Those who are disturbed by this, let them be calmed. 5D] BODHICARYAVATARA 127 Therefore, with respect to the body, one should cultivate the mind with such reverence. 'Om, let one cultivate the respect for the banner of Vajra according to the prescribed method.' 46 'Let not the joy be diminished.' 6. SATAN. 'In the region of birth, that practice indeed destroys the suffering of sin.' Moreover, the duration of the body is not accomplished. 48 100. In this line, it is translated according to Parijikd.

The Bodhicaryavatara states: "This world is not easy to attain through the practice of the path. Therefore, one should strive diligently, just as a person does not wish to be defeated. No one else can do this for me; how can I remain idle? If I do not act, may my pride perish. Even a crow, having reached the peak of a mountain, can soar like a Garuda. If my mind is weak, even a small obstacle can trouble me. The difficulties that arise are indeed easy to overcome. However, one who is engaged in effort, even if they are great, can be defeated."

Therefore, with a focused mind, I will act to avert calamity. Observing the victorious ones, I will laugh at the misfortunes of those who are afflicted. All beings indeed are conquered by me; none can conquer me. Thus, I, the son of the Lion of the Jinas, will grow. Those who are not respected by the victorious beings are not worthy of respect. Those who are not human beings are not worthy of being called human. By the power of my virtue, I will not be defeated. The foolish servants of the great ones are indeed defeated by their own ignorance.

The Bodhicaryavatara states: "In the world, how can one engage in actions without attachment? Therefore, even at the end of actions, one should not be attached to the results. Just as in the heat of midday, one should grasp the essence of the practice. One should not abandon the path of virtue, even if faced with the destruction of strength. Thus, one should skillfully arrange the means to overcome obstacles, like a warrior in battle, and should be trained by the wise."

BODHICAKYAVA'TAKA 155 11 The travelers, afflicted by the hardships of the journey, do not even rest for a year, those who are intent on their purpose. 74 150 The mind, being deluded by desire, does not attain the ultimate state. 75 The nature of beings who are always engaged in the essence of transformation is that they are often lost in the paths of others. 76 The Tibetan translator wrongly translates the Sanskrit word vayuh for ayuh. Its meaning is véyuh. But, Jaischke in his Tibetan-English Dictionary, p. 537, mentions that "सुर्यो सव स" is generally used in the sense of impelled or pushed on by the wind of actions.

The Bodhicaryavatara states that those who are foolishly attached to desires will suffer the consequences of their actions. Some are pierced like a spear, while others are burned by the flames of their own desires. In the face of the destruction of their desires, they remain entangled in their own suffering, unable to find liberation. Thus, the wise should avoid the path of desire, as it leads only to pain and bondage.

The Bodhicaryavatara teaches that one should generate a sense of joy through discernment, especially in tranquil forested areas devoid of strife and effort. Blessed are those who delight in the beautiful grounds beneath the moonlight, where gentle breezes stir the silent woods. In such places, one can reflect deeply and contemplate. In the healing abode, one should be mindful of the right time, and with care, one should act appropriately, free from distress and attachment.

BODHICARYAVATARA: Even if there is suffering in other beings, it does not affect me. Yet, that suffering is indeed difficult for me to bear. <9 [64] Without the pleasure of the beloved, even if there is suffering in others, it is still difficult for me to bear that suffering as if it were my own. <9 Even if there is suffering that is not perceived by me, it is still difficult for me to bear that suffering as if it were my own. <9 When my happiness is equal to that of others, what special quality does my own happiness have that it should be pursued? <5

When there is no fear or sorrow for others, then what is the special concern for oneself that I should protect it and not others? If it does not hinder me, then why should I be concerned? If I am afflicted by the sorrows of others, who protects me from that? The vision of the pure is not obstructed. This is indeed a conception that is established in the mind. It is different from that which is born from another.

If indeed the protection is for that very thing, then that is its opinion. Why is there no pain in the feet? That is because of that. Even if there is a sequence of support, I will be in the state of ego. Whatever is suitable, let it be as strong as possible. The progeny is like a powerful being. Whose pain is that? Whose will it be in the future? The pain is indeed more for the owners. Due to the nature of pain, what rule has been established there?

164 BODHICARYAVATARA 0 1 Noting the direction, thus the offspring of the enlightened, who are dear to the suffering of others... like swans at the end of the lake of the lotus grove. 107 In that essence. When the joyful ones are released, they are like the ocean of delight... 225 Therefore, what is the use of being liberated by the one who is fond of the essence? 108 Thus, there is neither intoxication nor astonishment in the higher arts. There is no expectation of the fruits of actions for those who have a single-pointed vision. 109 1. The Skt. Slokas Nos. 109-186 are supplied from the Journal of the Buddhist Text Society, Part II, 1894, A. - 22-25, edited by Sarat Chandra Das.

Therefore, I protect my own self with the mind that is filled with compassion, and I do the same for others. In other beings, the knowledge that "I am" arises even in the absence of truth. Thus, the knowledge of the self, which is inherently flawed, also applies to others. The abandonment of the notion of self and the giving up of the self should be contemplated.

BODHICARYAVATARA If I serve, what will I eat? This is the nature of the spirit. If I eat, what will I give? This is the nature of the divine king. 125. In hell and other places, one is cooked. By taking oneself as food, one attains great wealth. 126. The one who does not eat is not nourished; by that, the self rises in glory. By transmigrating to another place, good fortune is attained. 127. The supreme command is to serve the servant. The supreme command is to experience the friend. 128. Journal of the Buddhist Text Society reads Yadi Bhoksye. 169 265.

Those who are suffering in the world, all of them desire happiness. Those who are happy in the world, all of them desire other happiness. Not even the name of the Buddha is attainable; how then can there be happiness in the world? One who is happy does not change due to another's suffering. The world that is seen is not attained; it does not succeed. One who is not able to go does not give happiness to a friend.

The Bodhicaryavatara, Chapter 7. 132 "Those who are deluded do not abandon the suffering of others, which is a source of great suffering for themselves. 124 "In this world, as long as there are terrifying sufferings, what is the use of clinging to oneself? 125 "One cannot abandon suffering by abandoning the self, just as one cannot abandon the sky by abandoning the clouds. 126 "Therefore, just as one should abandon one's own suffering, one should also abandon the suffering of others."

172 BODHICARYAVATARA 1. My mind is determined to be connected with the essence of reality. Abandoning my own existence, I do not think of anything else now. 2. Not suitable is the view of self-interest; it is indeed the cause of suffering. Not suitable is the attachment to the self, nor to anything else through actions and so forth. 3. May I not be disturbed by the suffering of others. 4. Therefore, whatever is done in this body, may it be for the benefit of others. 5. Thus, whatever I may encounter, may I act for the welfare of others. 6. The self-nature of all beings is the same; even in the ultimate reality, there is no distinction. 7. [69]

The text appears to be a mixture of Sanskrit and some other characters, possibly containing errors or encoding issues. However, I will translate the coherent Sanskrit portions to the best of my ability: "Mayopama, the virtuous, how can it be in the state of goodness? If the illusion is real, what is reborn? The illusion of the mind does not exist due to the absence of the mind. The mind is indeed like an illusion, but the sins and merits are purified. The illusion is of various kinds and arises from various perceptions."

The Bodhicaryavatara states that there is no equality in the perception of all things. If one were to exist in the world, then what would be the purpose of Bodhicitta? The name of perception is not cut off by illusion; it does not happen. The separation of perceptions does not allow for unity; when there is no confusion, illusion is not perceived. It seems that, in the Bibliotheca Indica edition, Poussin reads this line in Sloka No. 14 according to the Patijika.

When there is no illusion, what can be perceived? The mind itself is the form; if there is anything else, it is not real. When the mind is illusion, then what is seen by whom? The Lord of the worlds does not see the mind. The mind does not cut itself off like a sword; thus, the mind. Just as the self-nature reveals itself, if it does, then it is like a lamp. The lamp does not shine forth because of the darkness; it does not depend on anything else.

1. What is seen is not dependent on anything else, nor is it independent of itself. 2. One should not think of oneself as separate from oneself. 3. The light of knowledge is said to illuminate, and the intellect is said to shine forth. 4. It is not seen by anyone. 5. Even when spoken of, it is like nectar to the barren. 6. Poussin mentions that Minaev reads here Nilahetur yatheksyate, but the Tibetan translation does not support this reading. However, the reading is supported by the Parijika. 7. This verse is quoted by Poussin in the footnote from Minaev's edition. The Tibetan translation of this verse is wanting. Anyhow, the Tibetan translation of the lines 8 and d is available from the previous verse.

If the body is not supported by the mind, how can one experience it? The suffering of heaviness is indeed present. There is also another satisfaction that arises from the purity of the mind. If the origin of suffering is not understood, then the imagination of pain is indeed present. Therefore, this consideration is opposed to the nature of the mind. The yogis, through their deep meditation, indeed attain the state of liberation.

When there is no contact with the sensation, how can there be the possibility of pain? What is this obstruction, and where does it arise from? If there is no knower at all, then why is this not known? Similarly, it is said that one does not experience oneself nor is one experienced by another.

There is no Vedic authority; it does not exist. The art that is without self is indeed bound by this. In the forms and in the intervals, the mind is not established. The unconditioned cannot be attained by anything else. In the body, there is neither another nor a being; nothing at all exists. Whatever is true is indeed manifested as the ultimate. If knowledge is to be known, what is its support? If consciousness is to be known, what is its support?

The text discusses the nature of knowledge and existence, questioning how knowledge can arise if it is known that it is not. It emphasizes the importance of understanding the essence of all phenomena and the implications of their existence or non-existence. The passage also touches upon the concept of certainty and the relationship between knowledge and reality, suggesting that true understanding leads to liberation from confusion.

BODHICARYAVATARA = The Way of the Bodhisattva. If the father is not present, the son is not born. In the absence of a son, there is no father; thus, the two are interdependent. The one who is born from knowledge is known as a being; what is not known cannot be attained. Knowledge arises from the seed of understanding; thus, it is attained. From where does knowledge arise? It is known through that which is to be known. The Vedas indeed state... The world is established in the essence of existence. The distinction of the path arises from the difference in the nature of the objects.

The ego is indeed a delusion. 0. What is seen in the presence of something else does not exist in its absence. In that reflection, how can there be truth? 145 290 Beyond the illusion...

The purpose of the existing state of being is to be known. What is the purpose of that which is not existing? When the state of being is not present, how can it be said to exist? When the state of being is not present, what will the state of being be? Indeed, with the absence of that state, it will be devoid of existence. In the absence of being, the state of being will not arise. The state of being does not become non-existent in the context of the absence of being.

The text appears to be a mixture of Sanskrit and other characters, possibly containing some errors or encoding issues. However, based on the recognizable Sanskrit phrases, here is the translation of the coherent parts: "May the fiery essence be present, and may the heated abode be there tomorrow. Let there be a gathering of worship, and may the auspicious ones be fulfilled. The fiery essence is to be sought. The battle of weapons and the battle of Pushya are to be engaged. All fallen beings, who are caught in the fire of the ocean of suffering, may they be liberated. May my auspicious state be attained through divine beings, and may the gentle ones be present."

The Bodhicaryavatara states: "May those who are endowed with great strength and those who are endowed with form, who are deformed and ascetics, be present. May those who are like gold in the world, who are virtuous, and those who are free from all sins, always remain."

The Bodhicaryavatara states: The mind of enlightenment is to be cultivated; those who are devoted to the Bodhi mind are free from the fear of the Buddha's wrath. May they always be happy and live long; may they never perish. May all beings be free from suffering. May they be like the moon that shines brightly. May the teachings of the Buddha and the Dharma flourish.

BODHICARYAVATARA 239 The Bodhisattva should not be disturbed by the various realms. May the glory of the sky be filled with the nectar of bliss. 26 May all beings, even those in the heavens, find rest in the teachings. 27 May they attain the gathering of the wise. 28 May the Lord of the world be worshipped by all. 28 May the divine be present in due time, and may the world be prosperous. May the king be virtuous. 28 May the glory of the teachings be spread.

May there be the power of the Bodhicaryavatara. May compassion be abundant like the Dakini and the spirits. May no one be unhappy, may no one be sinful, may no one be afflicted by disease, may no one be disturbed by any obstacles. May the assembly of practitioners be well established, may they always be in harmony, and may they achieve their goals. May there be the attainment of wisdom, may the practitioners be free from distractions. May they engage in their actions with a focused mind, free from all disturbances.

Bodhicaryavatara: May all the fully awakened ones be revered in many ways. May all beings be happy and may they be free from suffering. "By whatever means I may be endowed with strength, I shall attain the complete essence of the initiation garments among all beings. When I desire to see, and when I wish to see something, I shall behold that very Lord, the one who knows the essence of existence. As long as I act in accordance with the principles of the universe, may I become that. The state of existence of the world is dependent on the state of my being; thus, the sufferings of the world are mine."

18 53070 p64 A. 2 44714 4 From the golden staff, in the form of a beautiful ornament, The liberated beings are gathered together. I shall lead this great assembly of the most splendid ones. In the place of the great assembly, it is adorned. (11/11) Let the wind of the foot of the lotus be auspicious. May the auspiciousness of the foot be established. Let the delightful clouds of joy be present. The dual connection and the clouds of joy are all manifest. 20 And the equalized beings are in the direction of the auspicious. The one who gives the divine fragrance, The equalized beings are together. The auspicious ones are like the stars in the sky. 21 In all the sacred places and in the images, May the offerings of the harvest and the rains flow continuously. [95] The one who is adorned with the divine fragrance. 28 With their own adornments, they are splendid. In the assembly, they honor the Jinas as they should. Thus, I honor the Tathagatas with offerings. 22 Let the auspiciousness of the assembly be established.

Page 25

1. I bow to the great sage Sarama, who is the embodiment of all virtues. 2. I praise the ocean of qualities, the one who is the source of all beings. 3. The praise of the three is neither for merit nor for longevity. Neither strength nor health nor bodily happiness for me. The essence of taste is derived from the essence of the body. Thus, one should attain the essence of happiness through the knowledge of friendship. What is to be enjoyed is the mental happiness desired. The movement of the mind is filled with joy. Even if one is killed by the weapon of fame, What is the happiness of those who are broken by the actions of others? In the sky, the moon shines. As the moon is distinct from the sun, So does the praise of the divine manifest in my mind. Thus, the essence of the heart is revealed.

In the city of Ananda, the goddess shines here. In the forest, I am either in a state of joy or in a state of sorrow. 1. The great tortoise does not taste the essence in the leaves. Thus, even if it attains the leaves, it does not become a great eater. 2. Whatever is attained through effort, that effort becomes a source of suffering. The essence of this is that the one who is self-controlled enjoys the fruits of happiness. 3. The one who is endowed with wealth, even if they perform actions, may not attain happiness. Only through action does happiness arise; how can one who is inactive experience happiness? 4. The one who is not afflicted by misfortune, even if they are engaged in actions, does not become distressed. 5. The one who is engaged in righteous action should not be disturbed by the results. 6. The one who is devoted to the path of righteousness does not suffer from misfortune.

Page 142

1. By desire, one wanders in the world, like a deer in the forest. 2. How can one, who is bound by the ties of action, attain liberation through the grace of Shiva? 3. Therefore, even at the end of actions, one should not be attached to the fruits of those actions. 4. Just as a person, scorched by the sun, seeks water in the midst of a desert. 5. How can one abandon the city of strength, when it is surrounded by the bonds of attachment? 6. One should skillfully cut through the bonds of desire with the sword of knowledge. 7. Like a person caught in a battle, who is tormented by the enemy. 8. The wise person should cultivate the mind, which is afflicted by the pains of existence.
